Synthesis and Crystal Structures of Alkali (Na,
Rb, Cs) Iron Phosphate Crystals

December 2025

Saehwa Chong
Brian J Riley
Mark Bowden
Joshua A. Silverstein
Nate L. Brown

Prepared for
the U.S. Department of Energy
under Contract DE-AC05-76RL01830

Pacific Northwest National Laboratory
Richland, Washington 99354

 Abstract
 Three alkali-containing diphosphate crystals of AFeP2O7 (A = Na, Rb, Cs) were synthesized
 through melt-crystallization. The crystal structures were determined using single-crystal X-ray
 diffraction (XRD) data. The synthesized diphosphates crystallized within the P21/c space group,
 and the structural parameters were compared to literature data of other AFeP2O7 crystals within
 the P21/c space group.

Tables vi

 1.0 Introduction
 The diphosphate compounds with A+Fe3+P2O7 have been studied for their application as catalysts
 (Gabelica-Robert et al. 1982), magnetic materials (Riou et al. 1990; Nasri et al. 2013), cathode
 materials for lithium batteries (Clark et al. 2012), and water decontamination (Regil et al. 2012).
 The AFeP2O7 compounds have been known to form as three different structural types (LiFeP2O7,
 NaFeP2O7-I, and NaFeP2O7-II) that are composed of P2O74- units and FeO63- octahedral layers
 with shared oxygens (Gabelica-Robert et al. 1982; Riou et al. 1990). For this study, single crystals
 of NaFeP2O7, RbFeP2O7, and CsFeP2O7 were synthesized. The crystal data of RbFeP2O7 single
 crystal has not been reported within the Inorganic Crystal Structure Database (ICSD), and only
 one study on RbFeP2O7 powder X-ray diffraction (XRD) has been reported to date. In this study,
 structures and unit cell parameters of three diphosphate compounds of AFeP2O7 (A = Na, Rb, Cs)
 are compared to literature data.

Introduction 1

 2.0 Experimental
 2.1 Synthesis
 Three diphosphate compounds of AFeP2O7 (A = Na, Rb, Cs) were synthesized using a melt-
 crystallization method. The as-received powders of A2CO3 (A = Na, Rb, Cs), Fe2O3, and
 NH4H2PO4 (ammonium dihydrogen phosphate, ADP) in molar ratios of 1:1:4 were mixed using a
 mortar and pestle set. The mixed powder was placed into Pt/10%Rh crucibles and heated at
 300°C for 4 hours to remove volatile species and cooled to room temperature. Then the powder
 was ground using a mortar and pestle set and placed back into the Pt/10%Rh crucible. The
 crucible was placed in a furnace, and the furnace was heated to 1000°C at 5°C/min, held at
 1000 °C for 24 hours, cooled to 700°C at 5°C/hr, and shut off to cool naturally to room temperature.
 The synthesized diphosphates were washed with deionized water using a sonic bath, and the
 crystals were recovered with vacuum filtration.

 2.2 Characterizations
 Suitable crystals were selected for single-crystal XRD and were placed on cryoloops in oil
 (Parabar 10312, Hampton Research). Each diphosphate crystal was mounted on a Bruker D8
 QUEST diffractometer with a microfocus Mo-Kα X-ray tube and a PHOTON II CMOS detector.
 Data were collected with a scan width of 0.5° in φ and ω with 2–10 s dwell times per frame at 100
 K. Bruker APEX6 software was used for determining the unit cell and integrating the frames, and
 absorption corrections were done using either SADABS (Bruker 2001) or TWINLABS (Sheldrick
 2012) in the APEX6 software. The structure was solved by the charge-flipping method and refined
 with SHELXL. The synthesized single crystals were also ground for powder XRD analysis using
 a Bruker D8 Advance diffractometer using scan parameters of 5–75° 2θ with a step of 0.02° 2θ
 and a 1.5-s dwell at each step. The optical microscopy (OM) performed with an Olympus
 DSX1000 SmartScope and a 40× objective using different imaging modes, including dark field
 (BF) and differential interference contrast (DIC). Scanning electron microscopy (SEM) and energy
 dispersive X-ray spectroscopy (EDS) were performed with a JEOL JSM-7001F microscope and
 an OXFORD Ultim Max Infinity (170 mm) detector, respectively. Several crystals of each
 composition were mounted to aluminum SEM stubs using double stick carbon tape, they were
 then coated with 2.5 nm of iridium Quorum 150T ES (Electron Microscopy Sciences, Hatfield, PA)
 and analyzed with the SEM-EDS system. SEM imaging was done in backscattered electron (BSE)
 imaging mode.

Experimental 2

 3.0 Results and Discussion
 3.1 Optical Microscopy Analysis Results
 Pictures of the different crystals are shown in Figure 3-1. The crystals were multifaceted plates
 and most of them had what looked like specks of iron-rich phases presented as debris on the
 surfaces as indicated by red coloring. The sizes and shapes also varied between crystals. All the
 crystals were approximately the same color and appeared mostly transparent with purple hues
 when imaged in BF mode.

 Figure 3-1. Pictures of different crystals from the sets from each composition including (a)
 NaFeP2O7, (b) RbFeP2O7, and (c) CsFeP2O7. The top row and middle rows are dark
 field (BF) images, and the bottom row are differential interference contrast (DIC) images.

 3.2 SEM and EDS Analyses Results
 The BSE-SEM micrographs and corresponding EDS dot maps are found in Figure 3-2. The BSE-
 SEM micrographs show homogeneous compositions without bright (higher atomic number) or
 dark (lower atomic number) locations indicating compositional variations. The EDS maps show
 even distributions of the expected elements across each of the crystals.

Results and Discussion 3

 Figure 3-2. (a) BSE-SEM and (b-d) EDS images of AFeP2O7 crystals showing (b) A = Na, Rb, Cs,
 (c) Fe, and (d) P elemental distribution maps.

 Additional SEM-EDS data is presented in Figure 3-3 showing measured vs targeted compositions
 for multiple locations for different crystals. In general, the measured compositions, which included
 at least 10 total measurements for each crystal that were averaged, fit very closely to the targeted
 compositions. The measured Fe and alkali values were slightly higher than targeted values and
 the measured P values were either slightly higher or lower than targeted. A caveat with these
 measurements is that EDS-derived oxygen quantification results have uncertainties, and these
 values likely skewed the measured cation concentrations where oxygen was measured below
 targeted values for half of the crystals. Thus, the measured values for the cations were higher
 than expected, but still close to targeted values. For the single crystal measurements, crystals
 without the observable impurities were used for diffraction analysis.

Results and Discussion 4

 Figure 3-3. (a,b) EDS data for two crystals measured for (left) NaFeP2O7, (middle) RbFeP2O7,
 and (right) CsFeP2O7 including the (a) full EDS-measured vs targeted compositions
 (at%) and (b) the inset for the gray shaded regions shown in (a). (c,d) SEM micrographs
 of different crystals analyzed. Several regions (≥ 5) were analyzed for each crystal
 using 30-60 seconds of collection time per region (50k–60k counts per second) and the
 data was averaged.

Results and Discussion 5

 3.3 XRD Analysis Results
 The final refinements (see Table 3-1) for NaFeP2O7, RbFeP2O7, and CsFeP2O7 converged at R1
      = 2.54% with a goodness-of-fit of 1.04, R1 = 1.39% with a goodness-of-fit of 1.13, and R1 = 2.52%
 with a goodness-of-fit of 1.04, respectively. The XRD patterns of NaFeP2O7 were the same as
 NaFeP2O7 from powder diffraction file (PDF) 04-009-4838 (Figure 3-4) with the NaFeP2O7-II
 structure. The XRD patterns of RbFeP2O7 and CsFeP2O7 were verified with RbFeP2O7 PDF 04-
      011-4656 (Figure 3-5) and CsFeP2O7 PDF 01-081-1343 (Figure 3-6) reference data, and both
 compounds are isostructural to KFeP2O7. The CIFs (crystallographic information files) and
 checkCIF reports for each refinement are provided in Appendix A and Appendix B, respectively.

Results and Discussion 6

 Figure 3-4. Powder XRD pattern of NaFeP2O7. Reference PDF is 04-009-4838 NaFeP2O7.

 Figure 3-5. Powder XRD pattern of RbFeP2O7. Reference PDF is 04-011-4656 RbFeP2O7.

 Figure 3-6. Powder XRD pattern of CsFeP2O7. Reference PDF is 01-081-1343 CsFeP2O7.

 The crystal structures of AFeP2O7 (A = Na, Rb, Cs) are shown in Figure 3-7. The synthesized
 NaFeP2O7 compound was composed of alternating layers of P2O7 units (Figure 3-8d) and FeO6

Results and Discussion 7

 octahedra (Figure 3-8e) and parallel to the (001) plane and isostructural with NaAlP2O7 or
 NaFeP2O7-II (Figure 3-7a). The Na+ cation is coordinated by 8 oxygen atoms (Figure 3-8a) with
 the average Na–O distance of 2.63 Å and located in the distorted cage formed by FeO6 octahedra
 and P2O7 units. The P2O7 unit is formed with slightly distorted PO4 with a shared oxygen, and P–
 O distance of the connecting bonds is longer (1.62 Å) than other three P–O distances (1.51 Å and
 1.53 Å). The crystal structures of RbFeP2O7 and CsFeP2O7 were isostructural to KFeP2O7,
 KAlP2O7, or NaFeP2O7-I (Figure 3-8b and Figure 3-8c), respectively. Each FeO6 octahedron is
 linked to five different P2O7 units by shared oxygen atoms (Figure 3-8d and Figure 3-8e). The
 channel is formed by FeO6 and P2O7 units along the c-axis, and K+, Rb+, or Cs+ cations are
 positioned within the channel. The bridging P–O bond is slightly longer than other three terminal
 bonds in P2O7 unit. For RbFeP2O7 and CsFeP2O7, both Rb+ and Cs+ cations were coordinated by
 10 oxygen atoms (Figure 3-7b and Figure 3-7c) and have a similar polyhedron shape. The
 average Rb–O distance was 3.06 Å whereas the average Cs–O distance was 3.17 Å.

 Figure 3-7. Crystal structures of (a) NaFeP2O7, (b) RbFeP2O7, and (c) CsFeP2O7. Unit cells are
 denoted by black boxes in the lower left corner of each figure panel.

Results and Discussion 8

 Figure 3-8. (a-c) Coordination environments for (a) Na, (b) Rb, and (c) Cs as well as (d) P2O7
 and (e) FeO6 units. Rb and Cs coordination are similar but shown with different
 orientations.

 The types of polyphosphate phases discussed in this paper are often found within phosphate-
 based waste forms containing Fe2O3 and alkalis. Alkali iron phosphate phases can be present as
 crystalline in multi-phase waste forms as single-phase AFeP2O7 (single alkali metals), as solid
 solutions of multiple alkalis (based on EDS maps), or as similar compositions but with different
 stoichiometries and different iron redox ratios (i.e., Fe2+ or Fe3+), e.g., LiFePO4, Li2(Fe2+)(P2O7),
      Na(Fe2+)(PO3)3, Li3(Fe3+)2(PO4)3, K3(Fe3+)(PO4)2 (Riley et al. 2023a; Riley et al. 2023b; Riley et al.
 2023c). These crystalline materials have a range of alkali loadings (on a mass basis) and tend to
 form in slow-cooled materials rather than in quenched materials. It is common that quenched iron
 phosphate glasses can be completely amorphous or contain phase-separated (immiscible)
 amorphous phases. However, the way that these types of waste forms will be utilized, they will
 be melted and poured into canisters or containers for storage, at which point they will undergo
 slow cooling profiles, and this is the stage where melt crystallization will occur. The formation of
 iron phosphate crystals can be detrimental to the residual glass because crystallization of these
 phases is destabilizing the remaining melt. This is commonly discussed in the context of
 borosilicate waste forms where the crystallization of nepheline (NaAlSiO4) will deplete the residual
 glass of glass formers and lead to a less chemically durable glass residual phase containing highly
 durable nepheline crystals with an overall poor durability (Vienna et al. 2017; McClane et al. 2020;
 Deshkar et al. 2022). The crystallographic data on the NaFeP2O7, RbFeP2O7, and CsFeP2O7
 crystals match well with other AFeP2O7 (A = Rb, Cs) crystals from the ICSD as shown in Table
 3-2 and Figure 3-9. The parameters evaluated included cell parameters (a, b, c, β), cell volume
 (V), and the cell density (ρ), which are found summarized in Table 1 for the current work and
 Table 2 for the literature data. The unit cell lengths increased with larger cations in the structures
 while β angles decreased. The cell volume and density of RbFeP2O7 and CsFeP2O7 crystals were
 close to literature values.

 Shannon (1976) so those data are not included in these plots.

Results and Discussion 10

 4.0 Summary and Conclusions
 In summary, we produced single crystals of NaFeP2O7, RbFeP2O7, and CsFeP2O7 where the
 single crystal data of RbFeP2O7 has not been presented in the ICSD to date as opposed to the
 other two crystals. AFeP2O7 (A = Na, Rb, Cs) compounds were synthesized under the same
 conditions, and NaFeP2O7 showed NaFeP2O7-II structure while RbFeP2O7, and CsFeP2O7 had
 NaFeP2O7-I structure. These materials show promise as potential long-term storage options for
 alkali metal fission products from nuclear wastes, so more data is needed on these to better
 assess their potential for these types of nuclear applications.

Summary and Conclusions 11

 ;
 All esds (except the esd in the dihedral angle between two l.s. planes)
 are estimated using the full covariance matrix. The cell esds are taken
 into account individually in the estimation of esds in distances, angles
 and torsion angles; correlations between esds in cell parameters are only
 used when they are defined by crystal symmetry. An approximate (isotropic)
 treatment of cell esds is used for estimating esds involving l.s. planes.
